As Ganga-devi silently approached, the Vraja-vasis bowed to her and offered their heartfelt prayers. And while their heads were bowed, unseen to anyone else, Ganga-devi offered her respects to Krsna.

The Vraja-vasis basked in the beauty of Ganga-devi. She then manifested two more arms, bestowing upon them with those lotus hands the blessing of fearlessness, and then she disappeared.

The residents of Vraja, now convinced that Manasa-ganga was indeed the Ganga of the spiritual world, embraced and glorified Krsna. Then they all bathed joyfully in her sacred waters.

That night happened to be the dark moon night of the month of Karttika, known as Dipavali, and therefore the Vraja-vasis decided to stay by the banks of Manasa-ganga to celebrate.

The men decorated their cows and pets, the gopis decorated themselves with their best ornaments, and Krsna, His graceful hands artistically decorated with turmeric, made His cows jump and play.

Nanda Maharaja had his men place many thousands of lamps on the shores around the entire lake, and then the Vraja-vasis worshipped Ganga-devi, floating tiny leaf-cups holding ghee lamps on her waters.

That night a great festival took place on the shores of Manasa-ganga, in which the people of Vraja heard and sang the glories of Lord Ramacandra, gave each other gifts, and enjoyed an opulent feast.

As the last lamps burned out and the Vraja-vasis retired to their tents to rest — lulled to sleep by the peaceful lapping of Ganga's waves — Radha and Krsna slipped away to meet under the cover of darkness.

**\* \* \* \***

It is said that the current of the Manasa-Ganga river flows so swiftly through Govardhana Hill that its powerful waves move even the splendid boulders that line its shores.

Boatmen must be very expert, therefore, in plying their vessels across the expansive lake, especially in the rainy season when thunderstorms are likely to appear at any moment.

One evening such a storm overtook Krsna's boat as He, disguised as a mariner, was ferrying Radha and the gopis from the Paranga landing at Cakra-tirtha to Brahma-kunda and Harideva on the far side.

The gopis were carrying pots full of different milk preparations, eager to offer them to Harideva, but when they arrived at the jetty they were disappointed to find no boats waiting on the dock.

Actually, there was one old and very rickety looking boat left at the far end of the landing, but there was no boatman in sight. Indeed, aside from a rumpled blanket lying on deck, the boat appeared empty.

Disappointed at this turn of events, the gopis were just about to return home when they heard a low moaning sound coming from the old boat, as if someone were stirring from a deep slumber.

Radha ordered one of Her manjaris, "Find out where that noise is coming from." The girl dutifully walked down the landing, gingerly stepped onto the boat, and cautiously approached the blanket.

From beneath the blanket came what sounded like heavy breathing. The gopi hesitated for a moment and looked back towards Radha, who encouraged the envoy with a movement of Her hand.

The young girl took courage and pulled back the dusty blanket. There, lying on the boat's deck, was a young boatman who, from the sounds of His intermittent snoring, appeared to be in deep sleep.

The gopi turned back to her curious friends and said in a loud whisper, "It looks like a boatman. But He's asleep." Hearing this, Radha walked down the pier until She stood directly above the boat.

She cleared Her voice and called out, "Boatman! Boatman!" He didn't seem to hear but let out a deep sigh of drowsy contentment instead, unconsciously pulling at the blanket in the manjari's hand.

"Boatman! Boatman!" Radha called again, but louder, and this time accompanied by a chorus of Her amused girlfriends. Finally, the boatman stirred, turned over, and then sat up, rubbing His eyes.

He was a young man, dressed in simple clothes with His hair tucked under a well-worn turban, but He must have been new to His profession because His hands and feet looked soft, not calloused.

The boy said, "It's been a long day and I'm resting. Please get yourself another boat or come back tomorrow." And with that He lay down again, pulling the blanket away from the gopi and over His head.

At Radha's signal, Her maidservant pulled the blanket away and Lalita-devi said in her assertive voice, "Wake up, boatman! The princess of Varsana wants to cross the river to worship Harideva.

"Do not delay. Set Your oar in place and take us to the far shore. You will be handsomely rewarded — not to mention the fact that You will have the privilege to serve the most chaste and pious girl of Vraja."

The boatman finally sat up. After much discussion and barter, He reached an agreement with Lalita that His payment for transporting the gopis would be Harideva's yogurt, butter, and ksira remnants.

As the gopis began to board the boat, their captain insisted that it would be safer to go in two trips, but Radha would hear nothing of it. Night was coming, and they were in a rush; they would all go at once.

The boatman reluctantly pushed off from shore, pushing and pulling his long oar from the boat's raised stern, all the while singing some song about boating on the river with one's sweetheart.

They seemed to be making good time, but about a third of the way across the lake the boatman stopped rowing, tilted his turban back on his head, and sat down looking out over the water.

The cowherd girls looked at one another. Radha then prompted one of them to enquire, "O boatman! Why have You stopped?" To which came the reply, "Dear lady! I am tired and hungry.

"You woke Me after a hard day's work. Before I could have My supper you filled this boat to twice its capacity and insisted that I race to the other side. Without eating I cannot exert Myself any further."

At first the gopis tried to cajole the boatman into rising to the occasion, then they threatened Him with retribution. When neither tactic worked, they gave Him access to their pots and He ate.

Unbelievably enough, the boatman devoured the contents of not just one, two, or three, but all of the pots on board, after which he declared, "Following such a feast, I must take a short digestive nap."

The gopis spoke heatedly amongst themselves — as the strong current of the river swept them out of sight of Harideva's temple. Meanwhile, their captain was stretched out on His seat having a snooze.

As if to pacify their irritation with good news, the boatman announced, "If two of you maidens massaged My feet and another two My arms, I could resume My rowing that much sooner."

The gopis stirred angrily to hear this outrageous proposal, and their moving about made the boat rock, then list to one side, causing the frightened gopis to shriek and the unusual boatman to laugh.

Finally, after the boat had righted itself, Lalita threatened, "Boatman! Unless You start to row right now, we certainly will hold You by the hands and feet, but only to toss You overboard. Now get up!"

Reluctantly the boatman resumed His service, and not a moment too soon, for clouds were beginning to gather. Before long, a strong wind whipped about the lake, and water began to seep into the vessel.

Shaking His head — and with it, the small peacock feather in His turban — the boatman called out over the gales, "I warned you that all of you together would be too heavy a load for this old boat. Now look!

"My boat is taking on water. Unless you lighten our load by disposing of some weight, we will surely sink." The gopis grumbled, cursing their fate as they threw their valuable gold pots overboard.

But even as the girls watched their family heirlooms disappear into the depths one by one, the boatman called out to them, saying, "The boat is still taking on water, you must discard your weighty jewellery."

In chorus the girls answered, "Never!" But as their skirt hems started to soak up the rising water, they tossed their necklaces and bracelets into the lake's swelling waves with tears of resignation.

But the gopis could see that the water level in the boat was still rising. They cast doe-eyed, helpless looks towards their intrepid captain, who shrugged His shoulders and advised them, "Travellers!"

The only way we can keep this boat from sinking is if you bail the water out yourselves." To which they answered angrily, "We could have done that with the pots You made us throw overboard."

The boatman suggested, "Use your blouses to soak up the water and then wring them out overboard." The incensed cowherd girls answered, "We will wring Your neck and throw You overboard!"

The gopis threatened the boatman with a host of punishments for His indecency and incompetence, all the while urging Him to wield His oar as the gusting winds whipped the lake into a veritable fury.

The waves rolled freely over the ship's hull and the clouds released a deluge from above as the gopis sat helplessly in their tiny craft, now reproaching Radha for having been so keen for this evening escapade.

When the preposterous boatman noticed that even a tempest could not dampen the arrogance of His passengers, He began to rock the boat from side to side, further filling the hull with water.

The gopis were now completely drenched and unwilling to stand for fear of capsizing the vessel. Sitting up to their waists in water still rising from below, and subject to torrents from above, they despaired.

Sri Radha now felt extremely guilty for risking Her girlfriends' lives— not to mention distressed at the thought of never seeing Krsna again. Having no other recourse, She stood up and raised Her arms.

Radharani sloshed past Her friends from bow to stern as She called out, "Please save us!" and threw Her arms around the boatman. At that instant, a flash of lightning struck Her.

That lightning flash did not come from the clouds, however, but from the body of the boatman, who, pleasantly surprised, clasped a free arm around Radha and pulled Her even closer to Him.

The seductive effect of the boatman's touch at once revealed His identity to Radha. But the gopis — who had never seen Radha even so much as look at a man other than Krsna— were shocked.

They cried out over the howling wind, "Siva save us! Just as we are about to sink to our watery graves in this lake, chaste Radha tosses us into an ocean of embarrassment by embracing a low-born boatman."

Rather than defend Herself — to the gopis wide-eyed disbelief— Radha reached behind the boatman and from His cloak, like a magician pulls a rabbit out of a hat, snatched a bamboo flute.

As Radha held up the flute, the thunder and lightning stopped, the winds died down, the clouds dispersed, the waters receded, and the gopis' faces lit up with smiles. "Oh!" they cried. "It's Him!"

And Krsna, as pleased with Himself as an actor receiving a standing ovation, bowed His head towards the gopis and pulled Radha even closer as She struggled to get free from His grasp.

The gopis had been terribly frightened by their ordeal, and they were still drenched from head to toe, but seeing Krsna standing there before them made all the torment worthwhile.

They were, after all, loving puppets in the hands of the supreme puppet-master, who pulls the strings of love at will, uniting Him and His eternal companions upon the stage of His vraja-lila.

Beneath the silvery moon Krsna steered His vessel towards the nearest island where He and the gopis changed into dry clothes while joking about the exhilarating highlights of their adventure.

Once again they boarded the boat, which had, like Krsna, cast off its disguise to reveal a golden, jewel-studded luxury craft equipped with all the accouterments necessary to serve the divine couple.

Then, as Radha and Krsna reclined on a comfortable couch, some gopis sang, others played musical instruments, and still others steered their boat through the placid, lotus-filled waters of Manasa-Ganga.

Thus the divine boat embarked on an island-to-island cruise (nauka-lila) in which Krsna and the gopis revelled in their mutual love within the transcendental beauty of Manasa-Ganga's opulence.

I pray that one day I may be blessed to sit by the shores of Manasa-Ganga and hear the sound of the gopis' laughter mixed with the sweet melodies of Radha's flute as they echo over its waters.

Worship Manasa-Ganga, where beautiful Radha once prayed to Krsna the boatman to save Her from a great storm, for which He claimed as His fee the fulfillment of His amorous desires.

**\* \* \* \***

Before Caitanya Mahaprabhu performed parikrama of Govardhana Hill, He followed the established tradition of first bathing in Manasa-Ganga and then visiting the temple of Harideva.

Before visiting the many sacred sites around Govardhana Hill, the king of mountains, devotees follow the Lord's example and immerse themselves in the soothing embrace of Mother Ganga.

I bowed down before Manasa-Ganga and, within my mind, took a holy dip before moving on to where Srila Prabhupada's spiritual master says the lotus mouth (Mukharavinda) of Govardhana Hill is located.

Although in Bhauma Vrndavana the locals claim several silas to be the mouth of Govardhana Hill, Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura has confirmed that the authentic location is at Mukharavinda temple.

Here devotees make offerings of food to Giriraja, confident that he will accept them, provided of course that they place a tulasi leaf on each offering and sprinkle it with water from Manasa-Ganga.

From Mukharavinda I proceeded to the corner of the eastern petal of the lotus of New Vraja-dhama that corresponds to the place in Vraja where Harideva, an incarnation of Lord Narayana, resides.

Lord Caitanya became mad with ecstatic love when He visited Harideva. He offered His obeisances to the Deity and then danced in a wonderful way that attracted the curiosity of all the local people.

The locals were astonished to witness Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu's ecstatic love and behold His personal beauty. The priests who served Harideva offered the Lord a grand reception.

That night Lord Caitanya stayed awake chanting at Harideva's temple. He lamented that He would not be able to see Madhavendra Puri's Gopala, another Deity intimately linked with Govardhana Hill.

The Gopala Deity stayed in a temple atop Govardhana Hill near the village of Annakuta, and Caitanya Mahaprabhu — thinking Himself a devotee of Krsna — was unwilling to climb the hill.

Lord Caitanya lamented, "Since I shall not at any time climb Govardhana Hill, how will I be able to see Gopala Raya?" Finding no solution to this quandary, He remained silent.

But Gopala knew what was on Gauranga's mind, and so He played a trick. He inspired a villager to spread the rumour that a group of Turkish soldiers was nearby preparing to plunder their village.

The informant told Gopala's caretakers, "You must immediately flee this village along with the Deity because the Muslim soldiers will come tomorrow and will no doubt cause harm to the Lord."

The villagers became very fearful upon hearing this news. They moved Gopala, along with their families and household possessions, to a village named Ganthuli on the opposite side of Govardhana Hill.

In Ganthuli, Gopala was kept in the house of a brahmana. For fear of the Muslims, His worship was conducted secretly, but because it was Gopala's desire, news of His whereabouts leaked out to the devotees.

The next morning Lord Caitanya woke up, took His bath in Manasa- ganga, and began to circumambulate Govardhana Hill, at times dancing and at times singing in ecstatic love for Krsna.

He recited a verse once spoken by Radha,

*hantayam adrir abala hari-dasa-varyo yad rama-krsna-carana-sparasa-pramodah*  
*manam tanoti saha-go-ganayos tayor yat paniya-suyavasa-kandara-kandamulaih*

**"Of all the devotees, this Govardhana Hill is the best! O My friends, this hill supplies Krsna and Balarama, along with Their calves, cows, and cowherd friends, with all kinds of necessities —**

**"Water, very soft grass, caves, fruits, flowers, and vegetables. In this way the hill offers respects to the Lord. Being touched by the lotus feet of Krsna and Balarama, Govardhana Hill appears very jubilant."**

As soon as Lord Caitanya reached Govinda-kunda, He heard that Gopala had been moved from His temple. And so it was that when He arrived at Ganthuli-grama, He got the opportunity to see the Deity.

Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu was at once overwhelmed by ecstatic love upon seeing the beauty of Gopala. The Lord recited the following verse and chanted and danced until day's end.

**Lord Caitanya sang, "May the left arm of Sri Krsna, whose eyes are like the petals of a lotus flower, always protect you. With His left arm He raised Govardhana Hill as if it were a toy."**

Lord Caitanya remained at the home of the Ganthuli brahmana for the three days Gopala stayed there. On the fourth day, as the Deity returned to His temple, Mahaprabhu went along with Him.

But once again, Caitanya Mahaprabhu would not walk upon Giriraja. He went only as far as the foot of the mountain. From there He offered obeisances to Gopala before continuing His parikrama.

In an earlier chapter we heard of a similar occasion when Gopala decided to favour the devotees of Vrndavana. At that time He travelled as far as Mathura, where He stayed for one month.

This is the way of Krsna's kindness towards His devotees. These displays of affection overwhelm the devotees with love, making them mad after their Lord. Who could be a better master than Sri Hari?

Who could be more considerate than Sri Hari? Because His devotees desire to see Him, He also appears to them in a form like that of Harideva, becoming their family member or worshipable Lord.

At the site where Harideva now stands, Srimati Radharani and the cowherd girls were once sharing amongst themselves the various encounters they had enjoyed with Krsna, as they so often do.

As they talked excitedly about Krsna's beautiful features, charming ways, and humorous words, they felt that He was there with them in sound but at the same time separated from them in form.

Very quickly, their feelings of separation intensified to fever pitch. The gopis could no longer speak coherently, and so they resorted to the sankirtana of His names, chanting, "O Hari! O Deva!"

Unable to neglect His beloveds' call in the pain of their sorrow, Krsna appeared before the gopis with His flute held in His right hand and His left hand raised as if lifting Govardhana Hill.

Once Krsna had appeared before them, the gopis fed Him delicious food, decorated Him with flowers, and massaged His limbs, but when it was time for Him to return to His cows they could not let Him go.

Radha pleaded in a voice that Krsna could not bear, saying, "Always stay here with us!" But He could also hear the gopas nearby, anxiously calling, "O friend! Where are You? Where have You gone?"

To satisfy both groups of His dearest devotees, Krsna returned to His cowherd boyfriends and the business of tending cows, but left behind a Deity of Himself for the gopis to serve in their own intimate way.

From that day on, the gopis came daily to serve the Deity of Krsna, whom they called Harideva, bathing, dressing, and ornamenting Him in the same way that they would serve Krsna in the kunjas of Vraja.

There was no end to the ecstasy Srimati Radharani felt at being able to freely serve this Deity of Krsna, whom the other residents of Vraja thought to be an incarnation of Lord Narayana.

## Chapter Twelve - Brahma-kunda to Gauri-tirtha

Brahma-kunda lies halfway between the temple of Harideva and Manasa-ganga. It was created from the water Brahma used to bathe Krsna in order to atone for stealing the gopas and calves.

Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu visited this place after seeing Harideva's transcendental form. While His servant cooked lunch, the Lord bathed there, then ate and rested beneath a tree by the lakeside.

Some years earlier, Lord Nityananda, accompanied by a local brahmana, also chose to rest beneath the same tree while He was enthusiastically circumambulating Govardhana Hill.

On the four sides of Brahma-kunda, beginning with the eastern and proceeding clockwise, are the lakes of Indra, Yamaraja, Varuna, and Kuvera, formed when each of these demigods bathed Krsna.

By affectionately bathing Krsna, the four-headed creator revealed the conclusion of his teachings — loving service to the Lord. Brahma-kunda is the liquefied form of that conclusion.

It is said that the four lakes surrounding Brahma-kunda sing the glories of this liquid form of the Brahma sampradaya's siddhanta, and that Lord Varaha always enjoys pastimes in the middle of the kunda.

Yogamaya resides on the bank of Brahma-kunda as Manasi-devi, the presiding deity of Manasa-Ganga, who is one of the four goddesses of Vraja, the others being Vrnda, Yogamaya, and Patalesvari.

The demigods just mentioned installed Manasi-devi after they had all bathed Krsna. Merely seeing Yogamaya in this form frees one from the reactions to all the sins he has committed in previous lives.

I took Manasi-devi's permission and returned to the parikrama path where I offered respects to Indra-dhvaja-tirtha, a small hill where Nandaraja and the Vraja-vasis annually worship Indra.

They would raise a flag (dhvaja) at this place in honour of the king of heaven and then worship him to appeal for abundant rainfall, ensuring ample grasses for their cows, their very means of sustenance.

Krsna brings His cows here to graze. When one or more of them strays, He calls them back by the sound of His flute, and they come running, eager for His loving caresses.

Once Krsna sat here, perched upon a large boulder. As He remembered the most recent sacrifice to the demigod, He gazed up at Indra's flagpole and said out loud, "Nonsense!

"This banner is nothing but the insignia of futility— the futility represented by demigod worship. And I have appeared to replace such futility with the timeless jewel of pure devotion to Me.

"At the next opportunity I will bring an end to this tradition of worshipping Indra and instead introduce the eternal tradition of worshipping Me through the worship of Govardhana Hill."

To the west of Indra-dhvaja-tirtha is Rnamocana-kunda, and to the east, Papamocana-kunda. These two lakes are famed for their sanctity, and devotees who bathe in them are freed from all vices.

In Bhauma Vrndavana both lakes have been filled in and lost to urban development. Papamocana-kunda is now Govardhana town's bus station, and Rnamocana-kunda is home to shops and residences.

As I continued to walk in an easterly direction, I came to the residence of Laksmi-Narayana, the festival Deities of Govardhana Hill and the beloved masters of Ramanujacarya and his followers.

Authorities who worship the Supreme Lord with reverence say that these Deities are actually Krsna and His queen Satyabhama, come to Govardhana Hill for a specific purpose.

That purpose, they say, is to attract worshippers in the regulated devotion of Vaikuntha to the spontaneous devotion of Vrndavana — through the hearing of Krsna's pastimes at Govardhana.

Thus Gaudiya Vaisnavas also pray, "O Lord Narayana! We offer our respectful obeisances unto You. Kindly be merciful to us. Give us the association of Krsna and thus vanquish our lamentation."

A nearby path crosses over Govardhana Hill. The peak of that pass is called Krsna-vedi, because here Krsna collects a toll from the cowherd girls for using this thoroughfare.

That place, in fact the entire area, is also known as Dana-gati because here, in the dress of a customs official, Krsna extracts taxes (dana) by setting up toll stations (gati) to tease the gopis.

These toll pastimes are rich with loving exchange. Once the rock upon which Radha and Krsna stood melted in ecstasy, leaving imprints of Their lotus feet, which are visible even today.

Vajra originally installed a Deity of Dani-raya on top of Govardhana Hill. A derelict and partly ruined temple, vacated during Aurangzeh's attacks, currently marks that hallowed spot.

Dani-raya was moved to His current location beside the road that crosses over Govardhana Hill. Every sunset an elaborate arati ceremony is offered to Giriraja in a garden opposite that temple.

Both Rupa and Raghunatha Dasa Gosvami's wrote books about the Dana-gati pastime. Those books are like cintamani stones spiritually greedy souls gaze at through aural reception. Dasa Gosvami writes:

"A blind person found this cintamani jewel of Radha-Madhava's dana pastime at the base of Govardhana Hill. That blind man prays that the followers of Rupa Gosvami be pleased by gazing at this jewel."

Attracted by the prospect of being transformed by the touch of this cintamani jewel, I recalled a very special joking exchange that took place by the foot of Govardhana Hill at Dana-gati.

One day, news spread throughout Vrndavana that Vasudeva had engaged Bhaguri Muni and other great brahmanas to perform a fire sacrifice for the welfare of his sons, Krsna and Balarama.

The site of the sacrifice was to be Govinda-kunda, and the brahmanas made it known that anyone who gave ghee for oblations to the sacred fire would be blessed by the personified sacrifice, the Yajna-purusa.

A parrot named Suksmadhi delivered this news to Srimati Radharani, who at the time was sitting beside Her lake — Radha-kunda — with Her gopi girlfriends, enjoying a conversation about Krsna.

When Radha heard that all the residents of Vrndavana were welcome to attend the sacrifice, She jumped at the opportunity to see Krsna. She gathered Her friends, and they all set out for Govinda-kunda.

The gopis carried golden pots full of ghee on their heads as they walked beside Govardhana Hill, thinking, "We will meet Krsna on the pretext of donating ghee," and, "Where is the Lord of my life?"

Meanwhile, another parrot—Krsna's spy named Vicaksanah — had informed his master that a convoy of cowherd girls headed by Sri Radha was making its way towards Govinda-kunda.

Krsna decided to intercept the gopis as they crossed Manasa-Ganga, and so He climbed Govardhana Hill with cowherd boys like Subala, Madhumangala, and others, stopping at Krsna-vedi.

At Krsna-vedi — also known as Syama-vedi— the boys cobbled together a makeshift toll station and dressed themselves as customs officials, with Krsna playing the part of the chief tax collector.

Krsna's physical appearance enchanted the minds of even the gopas, who stood and marvelled at the striking combination of orange dhoti, red belt, yellow shawl, blue turban, and vaijayanti garland.

Krsna's ankle bells, rings, necklaces, and bracelets sparkled. And as Subala placed the solid gold collector's rod in Krsna's hand, Madhumangala exclaimed, "That does it! Friend! You look perfect."

At just that moment the gopis landed at the pier by Harideva's temple. Their constant telling of Krsna's pastimes caused flower-like symptoms to appear on the vine-like limbs of Sri Radha's body.

Suddenly, the sound of Krsna's flute interrupted their talks. The maidens of Vraja fell silent and spontaneously unmoored their minds, setting them adrift in the direction of Krsna's flute-song.

Sri Radha was unable to contain Her ecstasy. She broke the silence to say, "O friends! Let us perform austerities in order to take our next birth in a family of bamboo trees, the highest birth of all.

"In this way, like the flute that repeatedly mocks us, we will be fortunate enough to constantly relish the nectar from Krsna's bimba-fruit lips. Then, finally, we will know true happiness."

As the gopis walked along the path that led over the crest of the hill, Vrnda-devi pointed ahead to beautiful Syama and the motley gang of gopas standing before their toll station.

Vrnda-devi said, "O girls! I have seen Krsna many times before, but never have I seen Him dressed in such an adorable way, and exuding such nectarean beauty. Have any of you?"

The gopis excitedly responded to Vrnda's query and expressed their minds to one another. But as She approached Radha remained silent,

Her eyes transfixed through Her veil on that vision of Krsna.

**\* \* \* \***

As the gopis neared the toll station they covered their faces and steadied the pots of ghee they carried on their heads, making a conspicuous effort not to look at Krsna in His eye-catching garb.

Subala called out, "O girls! What are you transporting in those pots? And what, pray tell, is your destination?" Without so much as arching an eyebrow the girls walked on, now abreast of the tollbooth.

Relishing the gopis' defiant stance, Krsna stepped onto the path before Radha and blocked the way with His staff. He challenged Her with His soft but playful smile, suggestive glance, and bluish aura.

Krsna said, "Dear girls endowed with youthful beauty who hold many pots of ghee on your heads. You must either pay Me tax for using this road or take some other route to reach your destination."

As Krsna spoke, Subala came to His side and completely blocked the path, halting the girls' forward progress. Confronted in that way, some very special ecstatic symptoms arose in Radha's heart.

Signs of pride, ambition, weeping, smiling, envy, fear, and anger — all born of the bliss of seeing Krsna — appeared simultaneously on Radha's face, and by their force She drew back from Him.

The unified effect of these ecstasies was as enchanting to Krsna as a mixture of yogurt, rock candy, ghee, honey, black pepper, camphor, and cardamom, and was as satisfying as contact with Radha.

O thoughtful Vaisnavas! While hearing how naughty Krsna obstructed Radharani's way, please bear in mind the faultless words with which our acaryas describe Sri Radha's ecstasies:

"May the sight of Srimati Radharani's kila-kincita ecstasy, which is like a bouquet, bring good fortune to all. When Krsna blocked Radha's way to the dana-gati, there was laughter within Her heart.

"Her eyes grew bright and were reddened by the flow of fresh tears. Due to Her sweet relationship with Krsna, Her eyes were enthusiastic. When Her crying subsided, She appeared even more beautiful.

"Agitated by tears, Radha's eyes were tinged with red, just like the eastern horizon at sunrise. Her lips moved with jubilation and lusty desire, Her eyebrows curved, and Her lotus-like face mildly smiled.

"Seeing Radha's face exhibit such emotion, Lord Sri Krsna felt a million times happier than when He embraced Her. From this it can be seen that His happiness is not at all mundane."

As the cowherd girls observed this exchange, the fish of their eyes, darting here and there, became hopelessly entangled in the net of Radha's and Krsna's outward displays of ecstatic love.

And when Krsna repeatedly accosted the enchanted gopis with the words, "Pay Me My tax!" they took to their own defence, leaving Srimati Radharani as helpless as She was enthralled.

Radha dismissed Krsna as one would shoo away a pesky mosquito, but He persisted, repeatedly confronting Her face to face and demanding, "O beautiful one! You must pay a tax for Your youthful splendour."

Finally, from Her arched eyebrows Srimati Radharani launched a forceful glance at audacious Krsna that caused Him to totter where He stood, as if struck by a volley of arrows.

At Radha's glance, Krsna's entire body shivered in ecstasy, and His mind floated away on an ocean of spiritual mellows. Seeing Him temporarily at a disadvantage, the cowherd girls remarked:

"Oh! Is there also a levy on youth and beauty in this fictitious kingdom of Yours, whose king is a certain debauchee whose sole pleasure comes from challenging the chastity of faithful housewives?"

Krsna struggled to regain His composure but still reeled in ecstasy as He replied, "All valuable commodities — such as youth, beauty, feminine pride, and ghee — are subject to tax at this outpost."

Radha ignored His demands and moved forward keeping a wide berth around Krsna, who stepped in front of Her again, saying, "Hot tempered girl! How dare You ignore a representative of the king!

"Are You so intoxicated by the arrogance of youth that You have no respect for law and order?" Krsna leaned towards Radha, towering over Her in the way a snake towers over the prey in its coils.

His eyebrows arched to their limit, His smile saturated with innuendo, Krsna added, "There is no point in running away from a black snake, for when it raises its hoods — "

Krsna stood up on His toes and expanded His chest to emphasise His point, " — and hisses at You, then You will become bewitched and liberated from your youthful feminine impudence."

Radha turned away from Krsna and answered Her beloved with a disdainful voice that could not conceal Her inner desire to submit to Him, just as a cloth cannot cover the fragrance of a rose.

She countered, "How can a black snake enchant its enemy, the she-mongoose? It would be better for such snakes not to venture into the open but rather stay in the safety of their homes."

"Enough of this nonsense!" Krsna exclaimed, grabbing Srimati Radharani's wrist. Lalita let out a shriek and then shouted, "Rascal! Take Your hands off of this goddess at once!"

Disarmed by this forceful reprimand, Krsna let go of Radha's arm, now full of goosebumps from His touch, and took a step back as Lalita-devi wagged her finger in His face, exclaiming:

"We have carefully bathed, anointed, and dressed our princess for the sake of this sacrifice, and now You want to defile Her purity with the contaminating touch of Your hands? Off with you!"

Radha hid behind Lalita to recover from the effect of Krsna's touch. And Krsna, reproached by the gopas' looks of playful derision for cowering in fear of a girl, righted His turban and centred Himself.

Just then a spectator cuckoo advised, "O friend! Just flatter Radharani with sweet words and then quickly collect Your toll." At this, Krsna placed His left hand on His left hip and smiled.

Still balancing the golden pot of ghee on Her head, Sri Radha gazed at Krsna from the corners of Her eyes, shyly covering half Her face with Her sari as He spoke to Her over the top of Lalita's head.

"Dear Radha! Please stand for a moment by this tollbooth and let My assistant calculate the taxes You owe for passing this way. And I kindly request that You make Your friends do the same."

Radha shot a look at the gopis that said, "Let's humour Him or we will never get out of here." And then She stepped out from behind Lalita and approached the tollbooth, which sat in the centre of the path.

"O scribe, Madhumangala!" called Krsna. "Truthfully record the itemised tolls as I assess them. If, out of greed, you do not record everything accurately then the authorities will surely punish you.

Krsna turned to Radha and said, "Please pay close attention as Madhumangala writes in his toll register. Don't quarrel. Just make things easier on Yourself and Your friends by paying Your toll."

Krsna then graciously presented a plate of beautifully wrapped tambula to Radha and said, "Please take one, or more, as You like. You can sweeten Your beautiful mouth with these while You wait."

For the next half hour Krsna listed, one after another, the items in Radha's and the gopis' possessions He deemed worthy of being taxed and then calculated the outrageous toll He expected them to pay.

In His singularly humorous way, Krsna entertained His friends and lovingly teased the gopis by demanding that the girls pay tax on their unlimited feminine charms as well as on the ghee they were carrying.

And since Radha possessed, far and away, the greatest endowment of youthful beauty, Krsna's tax assessment for Her far exceeded the profuse wealth of the demigods stored in Kuvera's treasury.

Radharani's beautiful face, for example, fetched a toll of one hundred thousand full moons, one hundred thousand blossoming lotus flowers, and one hundred thousand jewel-studded mirrors.

The arrow of Her sidelong glance was valued at one million sapphire arrows, Her two beautiful eyes at one million emerald lotus flowers, and Her chin, at thousands of precious gems.

Her forehead, shining like a golden half-moon, was taxed at a rate of one hundred thousand moonstones, and the tilaka markings on that half-moon, at one hundred million pendants studded with sapphires.

But Her auspicious name, "Radha"— the sweet nectar that bestows ecstasy upon the residents of all universes and makes the hairs on their bodies stand up with bliss — caused Krsna to deliberate carefully.

What could a suitable payment for Her name be? After much thought, Krsna said, "My dear Radha! The tax on Your name is that You must now give Me the splendid jewel of playful pastimes."

After Krsna had fully assessed Srimati Radharani's opulences, He turned His attention to Her friends and demanded from them payments proportionate to their personal wealth.

And finally, for the many pots of ghee intended for sacrifice, Krsna demanded a paltry sum: five heaps of sparkling diamonds for every container carried by each and every damsel of Vraja.

When Krsna had completed His assessment and Madhumangala had added up the total, beautiful Radha glanced crookedly at crooked Krsna. She smiled with playful anger as She spoke:

"Friends! I will not travel any further on this path, which has become polluted by this seducer of young girls. Ignoring My words of warning, Lalita has foolishly placed Me in this rascal's hands. Now let us go."

Moon-faced Radha once again tried to walk away from the toll station with Her friends, but Krsna and the gopas, domineeringly wielding their toll-keeper's rods, would not let them pass.

**\* \* \* \***

Lalita again confronted Krsna, her face stern but her eyes smiling with pleasure, "Who in Vraja has ever heard of a tax on ghee? It is simply a sham devised by the king of bandits to cheat innocent girls.

"Friends! If you wish to rescue yourselves from the cold coils of this black snake, then go to the queen of Vraja and recount everything that has happened here. Then this serpent will leave us in peace."

With a slight smile Krsna spoke these crooked words to Lalita and the other cowherd girls, "My dear gopis! Please do not pointlessly quarrel in this way. Rather you should dutifully pay your toll.

"If great King Kamadeva hears that you have refused to pay the toll, he will become angry and punish you severely. At that time I will not be responsible for the suffering he inflicts upon you.

"However," added Krsna in a conciliatory tone, directly addressing Radha, "if You cannot pay this toll immediately but wish to pay Me at some other time, then You must leave Lalita here as collateral."

"That we cannot do!" the gopis shrieked. Krsna replied, "But if you leave without My permission, the king will issue warrants for your arrest, and then you will have to pay a fine in addition to your tax."

Turning to Radha, the gopis said, "You remain here and renegotiate the toll with this customs official. We shall collect some coins at the sacrifice and on the way back we can pay our dues and retrieve You."

Radharani was offended by this suggestion. Her eyes flashed red. Ready to abandon both Her friends and Krsna, She replied, "Apparently our friendship can be traded for a few copper coins.

"You girls may feel free to bargain your way out of some imaginary financial debt, but I, for one, have no taste for extortion. I shall now go to the sacrifice to be with more high-minded souls.

"Besides," concluded Radha, "since this king we hear about is no less imaginary than his kingdom, these toll officers and the toll they want to extract are all figments in the same fantasy. I am leaving."

Before Radha could escape, Krsna stepped in front of Her again, "O delicate girl! I am simply doing My duty to the king of this forest, whose orders cannot be disregarded. Please co-operate."

Visakha asked, "Is this King Kamsa whom You speak of?" Krsna emphatically replied, "No! It is the person whose sidelong glance makes Kamsa and his demons tremble and whose name is Kamadeva."

The gopis looked at each other, dumbstruck, while Madhumangala laughed, "How amazing! These ignorant girls have never heard of mighty King Kamadeva whose hillside capital is Pramada-manjan.

"Nor have they heard of the king's council of ministers, which includes Madhumangala, Subala, Vijaya, and a legion of others. And what about the king's pastime place, his host of glorious gardens?"

As the boys good-naturedly jeered the gopis, Lalita took up the gauntlet and challenged, "To claim that this Kamadeva is king of Vrndavana is ridiculous. Everyone knows that Radha is sovereign."

At these words the cowherd girls cheered, "Jaya Radha!" as Lalita continued, "Every last one of you attended the ceremony at which our Srimati Radharani was crowned queen of Vrndavana."

Visakha concluded, "Therefore, what is the question of Radha and Her attendants having to pay a toll to a mere vassal. Rather, you boys should pay Her a toll for the privilege of playing in Her forest."

"Ho! Ho!" bellowed Madhu. "Just see! These tricky girls are trying to turn the tables on us." Subala interjected, "But it won't work. O dazed Visakha! You babble on an on without even approaching the truth."

Krsna addressed Radha, "You may be the queen of one forest, but Kamadeva is the king of twelve. Thus, as a subordinate princess under the dominion of a superior emperor, You must pay up."

Visakha ignored Krsna and responded sarcastically to Subala, "If I do not know the truth, then you tell me what it is." Subala replied arrogantly, "I will avoid confusing you with a lengthy explanation.

"Instead—just listen — I will tell you the essence of the truth. Please know that the great King Kamadeva has assumed the form of my dear friend Krsna. The two have become one person."

Arjuna continued, "But that's only a fraction of our friend's glory. The Upanisads declare the forests of Vraja to belong to Krsna. They also identify Him as the reigning king of Gokula. Need we say more?"

Vrnda interrupted, "Ancient scriptures may have called Krsna the king of Vraja, but the rules of logic state that Radharani's recent coronation as queen takes precedent over archaic tomes."

Madhumangala countered, "Stop talking so much! Our dear friend Krsna is the king of this forest. Therefore you, the toll-paying citizens, should give all honour to us, His royal entourage."

As the gopas and gopis argued back and forth, and as Radha and Krsna delighted each other with covert exchanges of love, graceful Paurnamasi, as soothing as the moon, appeared in their midst.

Both the boys and girls offered their respects to her. Taking shelter of Vraja's guru, Radha sighed, "O lady! Now that you have arrived, we can, by God's grace, reach the far shore of this ocean of suffering."

But Paurnamasi surprised everyone by seeming to take Krsna's side. "Since you girls stubbornly refused to pay a toll to the king of this forest circle, how can you reasonably expect to avoid suffering.

"This mock-quarrel has made peacock-feathered Krsna hostile to you all. As long as He does not win His beloved, He will remain stubborn and arrogant, and you will all be held up at this place."

Overjoyed at heart but feigning distress, Radha fell at Paurnamasi's feet and exclaimed, "O virtuous lady! Please don't leave us in such a precarious situation, at the mercy of that harsh toll collector."

Smiling, the brahmani embraced Radha with both arms and said, "Child! Don't cry!" And to Krsna she said, "O great performer of clever pastimes! Stop teasing these innocent girls."

Krsna bowed His head in consent and replied, "Whatever you order, I will do. Indeed, if you wish, I will personally pay the toll owed to King Kamadeva by these arrogant cowherd girls."

Paurnamasi replied, "Hari! Why bother Yourself today with this petty toll business? Allow these beautiful girls to continue on their way and deliver their ghee-filled vessels to the auspicious sacrifice.

"Tomorrow the girls will meet You at this same place. Then, in a grove at that lake nearby they will pay their toll in full, beginning with Radha. But for the time being, let my word of honour be their bail."

Hearing these words — as inviolable as the order of the Supreme Lord — the gopis giggled and covered their faces while Srimati Radharani playfully buried Her face in Paurnamasi's shoulder.

The gopas, feeling vindicated by Paurnamasi's implicit support for their arguments and intoxicated by having achieved their goal, embraced each other in triumph and began to pack up the tollbooth.

Krsna, palms joined, said to Paurnamasi, "By treading the path of your mercy, I have received the gift of this beautiful cintamani jewel, Radha, and the dazzling gems of Her girlfriends. I am in your debt."

Paurnamasi held embarrassed Srimati Radharani close to her side and, seizing the opportunity, said, "O Krsna! You jester! If You truly feel indebted to me, then please fulfil my two requests."

Krsna bowed His head in agreement as Yogamaya continued, "At this very sacred place, Dana-gati, please eternally enjoy Your toll- pastimes with Srimati Radharani and Her friends.

"Also, O Krsna! For those who live in Radha's forest, renouncing everything but the desire to one day directly serve the two of You in Vraja, please make the creeper of their ambition quickly bear fruit."

Carefully noting Paurnamasi's words, Krsna again bowed His head in consent and then, surrounded by His affectionate friends, gave permission for the cowherd girls to proceed.

Radha and the gopis then considered all their desires fulfilled, and so they happily left for Govinda-kunda, keeping Krsna in the corners of their eyes, their faces decorated with faint smiles.

Srimati Radharani was the last girl to leave. As She gracefully turned Her neck to gaze at Krsna from the corners of Her eyes, Her entire body tossed in the waves of the ocean of amorous love.

For as long as the gopis were still visible, the greedy bumblebees of Krsna's eyes drank the sweet honey of their faces. And when they were finally out of sight, His mind returned to the gopas and cows.

As Krsna herded His cows on the summit of Govardhana Hill, He again relished joking with the gopis by recounting the day's events to His friends. In this way, He and the gopas tasted divine bliss.

Meanwhile, Radha and the gopis arrived at Govinda-kunda. Their conversation filled the ten directions with sweetness and the great sages present there observed something very wonderful.

The sages saw the golden hue of Radha's complexion spread its lustre in all directions. Simultaneously, the ether was made white by Her soft smile and dark blue by Her lively sidelong glances.

The gopis offered their respectful obeisances to the sages, gave the ghee to those venerable brahmanas, and received many beautiful ornaments and other valuables from them in return.

After this, the damsels of Vraja, radiantly beautiful and adorned with the jewellery they had received from the unmatched sages, returned to Radha-kunda, joyfully discussing their toll-pastimes with Krsna.

Before leaving Krsna-vedi, I bowed down to recite a heartfelt prayer given by Raghunatha Dasa Gosvami: "Radha and Krsna are like two blue and golden jewels in the nectar ocean of the dana-keli pastime.

"They delight the hearts of Their friends. Although I am greedy for material things, and although I am spiritually blind, I nevertheless yearn to someday see Them face to face. "He also has written:

*Ghatta-krida-kutukita-mana nagarendronavino dani bhutva madana- nrpater gavya- dana-cchalena / yatra pratah sakhibhir abhito vestitah samrurodha sri-gandharvam nija-gana-vrtam naumi tam krsna-vedim*

**"One day, Sri Krsna, the youthful king of amorous heroes, became eager at heart to enjoy the dana-keli pastime. Blocking the path, He forbade Srimati Radharani and Her gopi friends to pass.**

**"Posing as a toll collector appointed by the king of love, He demanded that She pay a toll on the milk products She was carrying. I sing the glories of Krsna-vedi, where He enjoyed these pastimes."**

**\* \* \* \***

Below Krsna-vedi at the base of Govardhana Hill is a glorious forest of many pleasant trees, fragrant flowers, and mellifluous birds, where Radha and Krsna enjoy Their amorous pastimes.

In that forest is a sacred lake of crystal clear water called Dana- nivartana-kunda, where maddened bumblebees hum melodically in their honey-filled hives surrounded by aromatic flowers.

It is there that cuckoos, intoxicated by nectar, sometimes warble sweetly, proud peacocks sometimes dance enthusiastically, and green parrots sometimes chatter as they eat ripened fruit.

Because the gopis promised to return (nivartana) to this lake by Govardhana Hill in order to pay the toll (dana) they owed to Krsna, this lake has long been called Dana-nivartana-kunda.

Devotees give another meaning for the words dana-nivartana, describing a pastime that took place in the forest known as Dana-nivartana-vana, which surrounds Dana-nivartana-kunda.

Near Dana-nivartana-kunda is ISKON Bhaktivedanta Asrama, a refurbished edifice that from its rooftop affords devotees a beautiful view of Giriraja and the places of the divine couple's pastimes.

According to local accounts, the gopis returned to Radha-kunda infuriated by Krsna's audacity. How could He stop Radha, the queen, and demand tax from Her, and in Her own kingdom no less?

They conspired to give Krsna what He deserved by repaying (nivartana) His request for a toll. And so it was that Lalita sent a message to Krsna to arrive earlier than Paurnamasi had planned.

The next day Radhika was at home beautifying Herself for Her meeting with Krsna. Meanwhile, Lalita, Visakha, and a few hundred gopis hid themselves in the groves around Dana-nivartana-kunda.

When Krsna and a few intimate friends arrived, eager for Krsna's meeting with Radha and Her girlfriends, the gopis accosted the boys and gave them a good thrashing with flower whips.

Bound hand and foot to trees by flower ropes, the boys were helpless in the face of Lalita-devi's fury. They could only hang their heads while their feminine rivals humiliated them physically and verbally.

And of course, Krsna, the so-called King Kamadeva, received as much punishment as all of His comrades combined. Only when He begged for mercy did the gopis finally stop demeaning Him.

Lalita-devi stood before Him — her feet planted in a wide stance, her arms akimbo — and fumed, "O king of vagabonds, thieves, and debauchees! What do You have to say for Yourself now?"

His head hung low, Krsna could only mutter, "I surrender." Lalita held His chin between her thumb and forefinger and replied, "You're like a materialist. You only surrender when there's no other choice."

With her hand Lalita shook Krsna's head back and forth as she said, "But You must give proof of Your surrender. Words come easily, but can someone as self-absorbed as You actually surrender at heart?"

Krsna nodded His head in assent and Lalita-devi, after gently slapping Him, said, "Every day You bring Your cows to graze in this forest without the permission of our queen.

"Aside from eating grass, Your voracious cows also trample flowers and uproot saplings, and You go home happily without even considering that You should pay some form of compensation.Lalita now pulled forcefully on Krsna's ear, "Don't You think that You should pay tax for grazing Your herd, plus extra damages for defoliating this beautiful garden forest? Don't You?"

Just as Krsna was moving His head in agreement, Radha appeared along with Her maidservants. She took in the scene and was about to save Krsna when She noticed Lalita's fiery mood.

Radha walked timidly through the forest, past the gopas bound to tree trunks on either side, to where Krsna was being interrogated. Her eyes were filled with both sympathy for Krsna and fear of Lalita.

But Lalita greeted Radha's appearance as opportune. With eyes flashing and face flushed with fury, Lalita-devi turned Krsna's face towards her approaching mistress and said to her captive:

"Here's Your chance. First admit that You are no king at all and that Sri Radha is the queen of this land. Then apologise for yesterday's outrage, and finally, beg permission to graze Your cows here."

Krsna looked into Radha's eyes. Externally He showed pain at being humiliated, but internally He tasted the bliss of being Lalita-devi's prisoner and Srimati Radharani's slave.

Surrounded by gopis with flower-weapons, Lalita ceremoniously untied Krsna's hands, feet, and waist, freeing Him from His confinement. Eyebrows arched and lips quivering, she said, "So?"

Krsna took His cue. He folded His hands, cast embarrassed looks at the giggling gopis all around Him, and declared, "Dear Radharani! In boyish play, My friends and I offended You yesterday.

"I may be the play-king of this land, but You are its true monarch. Please forgive Me for requesting that You pay our play-toll. Please, just look upon Me and My friends as Your humble servants.

"Persecuted by demons, My family has had to move twice in search of green pastures for our cows and peace for our families. Therefore, please accept us as refugees in Your kingdom.

"If, in the course of tending our cows and executing our dharma, our herds cause harm to Your forest, please tolerate it as a necessary evil. Otherwise, we cowherd men will have no means of subsistence."

Krsna's words softened the gopis' hearts and brought tears to Radha's eyes, but Lalita, whose only desire, however hidden, was to intensify the loving exchange between the divine couple, was not yet satisfied.

Taking a flower whip from the hands of one of her assistants she ordered, "If you meant everything that You said, then show Your remorse and Your subservience. Bow before Your queen."

Again, Krsna looked around, not only to the gopis but to His shackled friends as well, who were all intently watching what would happen next. Then, with a smile that He could not keep hidden, He kneeled.

With His palms joined in front of His chest and His eyes fixed upon the spotlessly beautiful face of His beloved, Krsna knelt before Radha and softly said, "I offer My obeisances to You, namo 'stu te"

Without saying a word, Lalita prodded Krsna with her whip, and Krsna, His heart soaring with ecstasy, placed His head upon Srimati Radharani's bejewelled lotus toes and again said, "Namo 'stu te"

For a moment Dana-nivartana-vana was completely silent. Neither gopis, nor gopas, nor birds, nor insects, nor wind dared make a sound, so transfixed were they by this confidential picture of perfection.

Here was the Supreme Person before whom great Brahmas reverentially bow, placing His head on the feet of a cowherd maiden whose spontaneous love makes Him think He is a cowherd.Here was the Supreme Proprietor of everything in both the material and spiritual domains settling (nivartana) a debt He incurred while jesting with the cowherd girls of Vrndavana.

Here was the Supreme Truth — the worshipable object of yogis, jnanis, and bhaktas — for whom the supreme object of worship was a beautiful young gopi whose entire being was made of love for Him.

Because Sri Krsna enjoyed the dana-keli pastime with His cowherd boy and girlfriends in a secluded place by the shores of this lake, it has become known as Dana-nivartana-kunda and Dana-sarovara.

The truths of this place remain hidden from those averse to pure devotional service. Therefore, only pure devotees have the power to understand them. I pray that one day I may reside at Dana-sarovara.

Devotees who also desire to see Radha's and Krsna's dana-keli pastimes should come to this lake, gaze upon Krsna-vedi, and recite the prayer glorifying Dana-nivartana-kunda.

**\* \* \* \***

From Dana-nivartana-vana I looked with reverence in the direction of the sacred places Parasauli and Candra-sarovara, where Krsna enacted His spring rasa dance with the nitya-siddha gopis.

Previously, on the full moon night of autumn, when Krsna invited the damsels of Vrndavana to enjoy a rasa dance festival with Him on the banks of the Yamuna, the gopis were of different categories.

Some gopis were Krsna's eternally liberated associates like Radha and Candravali, but other gopis had been liberated sages, perfected devotees, or daughters of the demigods in previous lives in this world.

And although that night brought great happiness to everyone present, because the gopis in each of these categories had their own devotional moods, the overall atmosphere of the festivities was mixed.

Sri Radha personally felt the love that She and the other eternally-liberated gopis usually expressed had been inhibited, thus the happiness they usually felt from the rasa dance was constrained.

Aware that His dearest eternal companions were dissatisfied at heart, Krsna arranged a rasa dance solely for them on the full moon night of the following spring by the banks of the Yamuna at Parasauli.

The same branch of the Yamuna River that flows through Yamuna-mata curves east to pass through Parasauli before again turning north towards Nandagrama, and beyond that towards Sonkh.

Out of curiosity, on that night the full moon rose higher into the sky than he usually does, so that the towering peaks of Govardhana Hill would not obstruct his view of the forthcoming festival.

The forests nestled between Govardhana Hill and the Yamuna River, as well the birds, animals, and trees residing there, all rejoiced in their own ways when they saw Krsna arrive at the sandy riverbank.

His bluish complexion shimmered in the moonlight, the peacock feather on His head danced jubilantly, and the gunja necklace on His chest swayed to and fro with His every step.

After taking in the beauty of the area, Krsna stood beneath an expansive banyan tree, a second Vamsivata, and played His flute in such a way that only the eternally liberated gopis could hear.

Because the flute vibrated the kama-bija, klim, the seed mantra of Cupid, the gopis' hearts were romantically aroused and their minds captivated by the promise of a long-awaited meeting with Krsna.

As the fair maidens of Vrndavana arrived, Krsna greeted them with charming words, put them at ease, and let them know that He had arranged this meeting exclusively for them, and for no one else.

Then, on the soft luminescent sands of the Yamuna, Krsna and the gopis blended the arts of singing, dancing, kissing, and embracing to produce an emotion that great devotee scholars call rasa.

And because that transcendental emotion is aroused during what appears to be a festival of dancing, those scholars call that form of amorous diversion the rasa dance.

Krsna and the gopis enjoyed each other's company, the sounds and sights of the forest, and the beauty of the full moon night by walking from one enchanting grove to another around Govardhana Hill.

As they headed towards Sonkh, Krsna detected some rivalry amongst the gopis resulting in neglect of Sri Radha, even though it was mainly for Her pleasure that He had arranged the evening's festival.

Krsna felt compelled to correct the gopis' false pride and to exalt Srimati Radharani's peerless position amongst Her competitors. Thus, when the entourage reached Paitha, He disappeared.

As expected, the gopis were grief-stricken. In fact, Radha was so overcome by emotion that She fainted, and it was clear to the others that Radha's state was a result of Her superior love for Krsna.

Try as they might, the desperate girls could not revive Radharani. One of the girls suggested, "Let us find Krsna and bring Him back here. His cool touch and sweet voice will surely revive Her."

Meanwhile, Krsna was hiding within a grove on the bank of a nearby lake — soon to be known as Narayana-sarovara — a short distance from the cave He had entered in order to lift Govardhana Hill.

Before long, the gopis arrived there, looking like a phalanx of soldiers. They saw Krsna silhouetted by the moonlight and cried out, "Just see! Here in this clearing is Krsna, the son of Nanda Maharaja."

Krsna heard the gopis' cries and turned to look. As soon as He saw the gopis running towards Him, He froze in fear. He had wanted Radharani to come alone to search for Him, not the others.

To conceal Himself from the gopis, Krsna instantly assumed a four- armed Narayana form. Thus when the cowherd girls crowded into the kunja they were astonished by what they saw.

Seated before them in that forest clearing was Lord Narayana in all His Vaikuntha glory. In His hands were the famous club, conch, disc, and lotus, and around His neck, a Vaijayanti garland.

The gopis whispered politely to one another, "He is not Krsna! He is the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Narayana." They then bowed down to the Lord and addressed Him as follows:

"O Lord Narayana!" the gopis prayed. "We offer our respectful obeisances unto You. Kindly be merciful to us. Give us the company of the son of Maharaja Nanda and thus vanquish our lamentation."

The glory of the gopis' love for Krsna was that even the vision of four- armed Narayana could not make them happy. Instead they begged from the Lord the boon of achieving Krsna's company.

After submitting their prayer and again offering obeisances, the gopis departed with the hope that their search for Krsna would bear fruit now that they had received the Supreme Lord's blessings.

Meanwhile, Srimati Radharani's maidservants had managed to revive Her and raise Her spirits. They encouraged Her to follow the example of the other gopis and seek out Krsna.

And so it was that just after the gopis had left Lord Narayana, Radha and Her maidservants appeared in that grove. And to joke with Radha, Krsna retained His four-armed form and divine insignias.

When Radharani happened upon He whom She thought to be Her beloved, She was saddened instead to find Lord Narayana, evidently reposed in one of His earthly playgrounds.

Radha could hardly conceal Her disappointment at not finding Krsna. Indeed, while offering Her obeisances She could barely manage to mutter "Om namo narayanaya!" through Her tears.

Krsna tried His best to retain His four-armed form, but Srimati Radharani's desire to see Her sweetheart was so intense that He was forced to revert to His original two-armed form.

Like the other gopis, seeing Lord Narayana did not alleviate Radha's distress. But unlike Her peers, Radha's love forced Krsna to reveal Himself. This is the unmatched glory of Radharani's love for Krsna.

Thus, at Paitha devotees meditate on this verse by Rupa Gosvami,

*rasarambha-vidhau niliya vasata kunje mrgaksi-ganair drstam gopayitum svam uddhura-dhiya ya susthu sandarsita*  
*Radhayah pranayasya hanta mahima yasya sriya raksitusa sakya prabhavisnunapi harina nasic catur-bahuta*

**"Prior to the rasa dance, Krsna hid Himself in a grove just to have fun.**

**"When the despondent cowherd girls came, their eyes resembling those of deer, by His sharp intelligence He exhibited His beautiful four-armed form to hide Himself.**

**"But when sometime later Srimati Radharani came there, Krsna could no longer maintain His four arms in Her presence. This is the wonderful glory of Her love."**

The other gopis soon returned to find that Radha had forced Lord Narayana to reveal His true identity, which they could not do. Thus again they enthusiastically praised Radha's superior love for Krsna.

Pleased with the humility of the gopis, Krsna then showed them the tree that He had bent to convince the gopas He would be able to lift Govardhana Hill, as well as the cave He had entered in order to do so.

Then, leaving Paitha behind them, Krsna and His beautiful retinue continued to take in the night-time beauty and opulence of

Govardhana Hill until they came again to Parasauli.

**\* \* \* \***

The route they took on their stroll is known as "the long parikrama path" around Govardhana Hill, but because 'Krsna and the gopis were so absorbed in ecstasy they did not notice the distance.

Upon their arrival, however, Krsna and the gopis felt tired, so they rested by the banks of Candra-sarovara. When they were completely refreshed, they enthusiastically began the rasa dance.

The gopis linked their arms together and formed a circle of pairs. Krsna then entered between each pair of gopis, embracing their necks on either side. He also entered the circle's centre with Sri Radha.

Upon joining the festival of loving exchanges, Krsna completely submitted Himself to the rasa dance itself, in which He, like each and every gopi, was a dependent participant with no free volition.

Krsna was neither the leader of the rasa dance nor even an independent participant, for He had transferred His potencies to the pastime itself, submitting all His independence to it.

Because Krsna was only an ingredient in the festival, the supremacy of the rasa dance is universally proclaimed. Krsna's other pastimes, His energies, even Krsna Himself, are all secondary to the rasa-lila.

The demigods and their wives crowded together over Vrndavana to witness the event, filling the sky with showers of flowers and the resonant sound of their kettledrums as they sang Krsna's glories.

From the demigods' perspective the rasa dance sometimes appeared like a brilliant sapphire set in the middle of a gold band, and at other times like sapphire and gold bands intertwined.

At yet other times, the dance resembled an exquisite sapphire paired with a flashing diamond, both fixed at the centre of a band appearing sometimes like solid gold and at other times both sapphire and gold.

The pious demigods who were fortunate enough to see the rasa dance more clearly stared in wonder at the incredible ways the gopis moved their feet and hands, their flashing eyebrows and playful smiles.

With their braids and belts tied tightly, their waists bending, their faces perspiring, their breasts moving beneath their garments, and their earrings swinging to and fro, the gopis shone like lightning.

And Krsna, with His facial expressions, mystifying movements, and flowing robes, was like a billowing mass of clouds that sometimes concealed the streaks of lightning and sometimes revealed them.

Just as Krsna appeared more beautiful in the gopis' presence, they also appeared more beautiful in His. In the same way, the gopis' dancing enhanced the rhythm of His songs, and vice versa.

As that dancing reached a climax, Krsna enjoyed the pleasure of the gopis' company by embracing them, caressing them, and glancing lovingly at them as He displayed broad playful smiles.

Yet Krsna's dealings with the milkmaids of Vraja were completely free from lust, like a child playing with his own reflection; these gopis were Krsna's own pleasure potency expanded from His personal form.

The wives of the demigods became agitated with lust by observing these loving exchanges, but the demigods themselves were simply astonished by the activities of the Supreme Lord.

In their astonishment, the demigods did not even notice that — by the arrangement of Yogamaya — an entire night of Brahma was passing quickly, a span of time in which they should have all passed away.

So absorbed in Krsna's mystical, rejuvenating pastimes were these devas that without their knowledge their duration of life was extended. Such is the transcendental potency of Krsna's pastimes.

Amongst all the demigods, the moon was most affected by the wonders of the rasa dance. Stunned in ecstasy, he remained suspended in the sky, unable to move for the duration of the pastime.

The lake over which Candra hung motionless and into which he cried tears of ecstasy is now known as Candra-sarovara. It is still visited daily by the divine couple and Their friends.

The gopis were overjoyed to be with Krsna in the same faultless, perfected manner in which they enjoy with Him in the spiritual realm, and thus their minds and senses were overwhelmed.

In that blissful state, the gopis' hair and garments were dishevelled, their garlands and ornaments scattered, and finally, exhausted from such intense endeavour, their limbs slackened.

Krsna observed the gopis' fatigue and lovingly wiped their faces, bestowing upon them His reinvigorating touch. Then, holding their hands in His, He entered with them into the waters of the Yamuna.

Completely refreshed by the cool embrace of the Yamuna, the gopis playfully splashed Krsna with water while the demigods showered flowers and watched as their Lord played like the king of elephants.

After their sports, Krsna and the gopis got out of the water, dried and massaged one another's bodies, dressed in their favourite clothes and garments, and concluded the night with a picnic of fruits and berries.

Raghunatha Dasa Gosvami shows us how much reverence we should display to places like Candra-sarovara: "Their hearts pleased by the rising waves of the conjugal mellow, madhura-rasa—

**"The youthful divine couple enjoys pastimes in the beautiful groves of Vraja, aromatic with many pleasant flowers and filled with many humming bees. Let us sweep these forest groves with our long hair."**

During their midday pastimes, which begin at Radha-kunda, the divine couple also come here to enjoy rasa dancing and honey drinking. Candra-sarovara is said to be a favourite of Candravali.

This beautiful lake is octagonal. On its western side is a resting place of Radha and Krsna called Srngara-mandira, and on the eastern side is a small temple housing the Deities of Radha-Candrabihari.

The esteemed poet Suradasa lived by the banks of this lake. And because he also left his body here, naturally his samadhi is also here. Great devotees tell the details of his devotional achievements.

They explain, for example, that once Sri Nathaji's pujari, who had not yet dressed the Deity that day, nevertheless requested blind Suradasa to compose a poem to describe Nathaji's dress and ornaments.

Suradasa at first hesitated, but then began to sing. The devotees present were shocked to hear, "Today Hari is not dressed but remains naked." Naturally, they were stunned by the saint's spiritual vision.

One day in Parasauli, the son of Vallabhacarya, named Viththala, asked that same poet, "Sura! What are you thinking about?" By way of a reply, Suradasa then composed what would be his last song.

He sang, "Krsna's beautiful eyes are like khanjana birds — full of rasa, very restless, and slightly reddened due to intoxication. Seeing this, my life cannot remain in the cage of this body any longer."

After finishing his song, Suradasa at once gave up his body. From that day, devotees have come here to pray for the inner vision by which to see Radha's and Krsna's eternal pastimes.

Other holy places in and around Parasauli village include: Sarikarsana- kunda, Baladeva's pastime place, and Gandharva-kunda, where the Gandharvas marvelled at Krsna's divine singing.

Also nearby are two cylindrical stones, the petrified drums upon which Indra declared the supremacy of Krsna, and Mohavana, the forest where the demigods were illusioned by seeing the rasa dance.

The only sure safeguard against the illusions with which Mahamaya tempts the residents of her realm is to be absorbed in the bewildering illusions caused by the perplexing wonders of Sri Krsna's ways.

I pray that the blessed forest of Mohavana may absorb my mind in Krsna's all-attractive pastimes, protecting me from the enticements of worship, wealth, followers, and the allurements of the opposite sex.

**\* \* \* \***

Annakuta-ksetra extends from Dana-nivartana-kunda to the village of Aniyora. It was in this most sacred area that Krsna and Nanda Maharaja led the Vraja-vasis in the worship of Govardhana Hill.

Because this area was filled with thousands of hills of cooked food like samosa, kachori, and puri, and just as many lakes of sweet rice and ksira, it is considered the "table of Govardhana Hill."

A materialist who offers food cooked by a pure brahmana at this place has his worldly desires fulfilled, but a devotee who makes an offering with love realises his innermost spiritual aspirations.

Even a great sinner who bows down before Giriraja and touches the dust of Annakuta-ksetra gets relief from his sinful ways, and through the company of pure Vaisnavas he attains Krsna's lotus feet.

Of all places on the parikrama path, this one is the most suitable for remembering how the Vraja-vasis worshipped and Krsna lifted Govardhana Hill, because it was here that those events took place.

On a peak of Giriraja above Annakuta-ksetra, Krsna assumed a gigantic second form, proclaiming that He was the personification of Govardhana Hill and inviting the Vraja-vasis to ask Him for boons.

This huge form was so well disguised that Sri Radha, who easily foiled Krsna's attempt to disguise Himself with four arms at Paitha, never even suspected that the Deity of Giriraja was actually Her beloved.

As Krsna, in the form of the Deity of Govardhana Hill, ate all the preparations offered to Him, He chuckled to think how cleverly He had deceived His parents, friends, and girlfriends — even Radharani.

He consumed hills of food and thought, "If My mother knew that I was the one eating so many cooked, fried, and baked preparations, both salty and sweet, she would never again call Me a weak eater."

During this grand offering, the stones of Giriraja at Annakuta-ksetra became soft in transcendental bliss. Thus, impressions of the plates, howls, and pots that had held the offerings can still be seen there.

It was on the eastern end of Annakuta-ksetra that the cowherd men made the arrangements to serve Govardhana Hill — cooking, filling pots, cleaning them, and refilling them, again and again.

But the Deity of Govardhana Hill extended His long arms, and before an offering was even cooked, or a pot was full, He had eaten everything and was already demanding, "Bring me more!" (anore)

Giriraja ate faster than the Vraja-vasis could feed Him, and in larger quantities than they could possibly provide, therefore Krsna interceded. Coming before the Deity with folded hands, He said:

"We are poor Vraja-vasis and have given You all that we had, as fast as we could. Please he satisfied with us." So the Deity of Giriraja drank water from lakes and rinsed His hands and mouth.

Then, raising His hands in blessing, the gigantic Deity said, "I am now satisfied!" and demanded that the residents of Vrndavana ask for a benediction, to which they all replied in unison:

"O great Lord! We have only one request. May this carefree cowherd boy, Krsna, live long and remain always happy." At this the Deity of Govardhana nodded in satisfaction and then suddenly disappeared.

From the periphery of Aniyora, Krsna addressed the gopas, "For many years you worshipped Indra, but he never appeared before you. Now, just see what happened the first time you worshipped Giriraja.

"He appeared in person and directly accepted everything you offered him. Doesn't this clearly confirm the superiority of worshipping him over worshipping Indra, or, for that matter, any other demigod?"

Aniyora is situated on the banks of the great lake Sankarsana-kunda. Here Lord Balarama and His friends frolic while their cows drink the lake's clear waters or rest beneath the trees on its banks.

A Deity of Lord Balarama presides over this area just south of Annakuta-ksetra. Five thousand years ago, King Vajranabha installed the Deity here on the western bank of Sankarsana-kunda.

To bathe in this lake is to bathe in Dauji's mercy. A pilgrim who bathes here, in addition to having his desires fulfilled, is freed from all sinful reactions — even up to the killing of a cow or a brahmana.

But rather than aspire for freedom from sinful reaction, pure Vaisnavas who bathe in this lake pray for shelter at Balarama's lotus feet, without which no one can enter the abode of Vraja.

Take shelter of the lotus feet of Lord Balarama! Earrings touch His cheeks and enhance His beauty, musk tilaka decorates His forehead, and a strand of gunja berries adorns His broad chest.

His complexion is as white as an autumn cloud, His garments are blue, His voice deep and grave, His long arms touch His thighs, and with His unrivalled strength He killed the Pralamba demon.

Raghavacarya took Srinivasa and Narottama on parikrama of Vraja and they set the example by bathing in every one of its sacred lakes, most of which were no more than water holes at that time.

We must have similar faith in the transcendental presence of the dhama, and where its pastime places are no longer visible to the eye, we must see those places with our ears by hearing about them.

By practised hearing and by the dhama's mercy, our existence will be purified. In time we will feel the presence of the holy places with our hearts, and ultimately see them with spiritual vision.

Nestled at the foot of Govardhana Hill behind Sankarsana-kunda is a very sacred place known to devotees as both Srngara-sthali and Gopala-prakata-sthali due to intimate pastimes that happened there.

Once Srimati Radharani came to Govardhana Hill for a rasa dance, but when She arrived at this spot Her friends insisted that She rearrange Her clothing, ornaments, and makeup before meeting Krsna.

Before setting out on Her journey Radha had, of course, been beautifully adorned, but by the long walk, the dusty roads, and Her haste to see Krsna Her fresh appearance had become slightly dishevelled.

Lalita held Her by the hand and said, "You must look Your very best for Hari. Please be patient and let us touch You up." At that, Radha's friends and maidservants sat Her upon a stone and set to work.

Because Srimati Radharani was again dressed and decorated (srngara) at this place, it is called Srngara-sthali. And the many coloured stones in the area bear evidence of this pastime.

On one stone, red finger marks can be found from the lac that was mixed to repaint Radharani's feet. Another stone bears vermillion stains from the kunkuma placed in the part of Her hair.

A third stone has a small depression, stained black from the collyrium that Visakha used to paint around the base of Radha's eyelashes so that Her eyes would shine like Krsna's blackish lustre.

Every stone on Govardhana Hill is special, but for devotees steeped in devotion to Srimati Radharani, the stones that bear the marks of Radha's srngara are the most special of all.

It is also said that during the spring rasa dance at Candra-sarovara, Krsna Himself ornamented Radha here. For this reason, Srngara- sthali is doubly dear to Krsna and His devotees.

The second name for this place, Gopala-prakata-sthali, has great historical import for Gaudiya Vaisnavas because it was here, as foretold, that Madhavendra Puri discovered the Deity of Gopala.

There is a long-standing controversy as to who discovered Gopala. The appendix entitled "Who Found Gopala — Madhavendra Purl or Vallabhacarya?" presents our position in this debate.

This form of Gopala is very special. It is the same form that Krsna revealed d to the Vraja-vasis while He held up Govardhana Hill during the week that proud Indra lost his senses.

**\* \* \* \***

Madhavendra Puri once travelled to Vrndavana. After visiting many places of Krsna's pastimes, he came to Govardhana Hill, the mere sight of which caused him to lose control of his spiritual emotions.

Mad with the ecstasy of love of Godhead, Madhavendra Puri did not know whether it was day or night, whether he was in a proper place or not, or whether he was standing up or lying on the ground.

Madhavendra Puri somehow circumambulated the hill in this state of divine madness. Eventually reaching Govinda-kunda, he took his bath, and beneath a tree in a garden by Indra-tila he sat down to rest.

While he rested there, a very beautiful cowherd boy with graceful, flowing limbs brought Madhavendra Puri a pot of milk. That boy placed the milk before the sage and, smiling, spoke to Him:
